Optimization details:
Function `make_p(coeffs, n)`:
```
i = np.arange(n)
t = i/(n-1)
raw = np.ones(n)
for m,(a,b): raw += a*np.cos(2π*m*t) + b*np.sin(2π*m*t)
raw = np.maximum(raw, 0)
s = raw.sum()
if s<=0: raw=ones
return raw/s
```

LP primal: variables x_j (j=0..n-1). Constraints: for k=0..2n-2, (f*x)[k] = ∑_{j=max(0,k-n+1)}^{min(n-1,k)} f_{k-j} x_j ≤ M. Objective ∑ x_j.
Since f nonnegative, constraints. Matrix A (2n-1 x n) is convolution of f (reversed?) with identity; each row length up to n. A is lower+upper triangular Toeplitz with row k entries f_{k-j}. This matrix can be applied to x via FFT O(N log N). It is dense (n^2/2). However LP with such structured constraints and objective can be solved by dual:
Dual: minimize M ∑_{k=0}^{2n-2} y_k subject to ∑_{k=|?|} A_{k,j} y_k ≥ 1 for j=0..n-1, y_k≥0.
A_{k,j}=f_{k-j} if 0≤k-j<n. So constraint for column j: ∑_{k} f_{k-j} y_k over k where k-j in [0,n-1] ≥1. Let ℓ=k-j. Then ∑_{ℓ=0}^{n-1} f_ℓ y_{j+ℓ} ≥1, for j=0..n-1. This is a Toeplitz/Hankel matrix T of size n x 2n-1. This is the same convolution with f reversed? T_{j,k}=f_{k-j}. Constraint T^T y ≥1.
Dual has 2n-1 variables and n constraints. Still large. But objective M∑y. The primal optimum value can be interpreted as maximum total mass under envelope M. Since M fixed, the dual y is a measure on lags with kernel f.

Implementation details for fast convolution of two arrays (f,g) using FFT:
```
def conv_fg(f,g,N,ntot):
    F=np.fft.rfft(f,N)
    G=np.fft.rfft(g,N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(F*G,N, norm? default no)
    h = h[:2*n-1]
    # FFT convolution gives cyclic; with zero padding exact.
    return h
```
For f*f use G=F.
Rounding: h may have small negative; for max, okay. For constraints, if negative due to error, not issue. Could `h=np.real(h)`.

def solve_lp(f,M,n,lags,mult=1):
    # active lags list
    best_g=None; best_obj=-inf
    for add_iter in range(max_add):
       A = build_A(f,lags)
       res = linprog(c=-ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=full(M), bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
       if not res.success: break
       g=res.x; obj=res.fun (negative sum)
       conv = conv_fg(f,g,N)
       viol = np.where(conv > M*(1+1e-9)+1e-12)[0]
       if len(viol)==0: return g,obj
       # add most violated or top batch
       # If lag already in active? due numeric, maybe tolerance. Choose viol not in active set.
       # Use a set for active for O(1). If all viol already active but conv>M due solver tolerance, tighten tolerance or stop.
       new_lags = viol sorted by conv descending top add_count (e.g., min(32, len))
       lags.extend(new not active)
       if len(lags)>MAX_ACTIVE: maybe add only most violated and drop? Can't drop; but cap. If exceed, break and use current feasible? LP implementation details in code:
```
def lp_best_response(f, M, n, N, lags, deadline, max_lags=4096):
    lags = list(lags)
    active_set = set(lags)
    g = f.copy()  # feasible
    obj = 1.0
    for inner in range(60):
       if time > deadline - 20: break/return
       A = build_named? use dense zeros (len(lags), n)
       for ri,k in enumerate(lags):
          if k < n-1:
             length = k+1
             A[ri, :length] = f[k::-1]
          else:
             start = k-n+1
             length = n-start
             A[ri, start:] = f[n-1:n-1-length:-1]  # check
       # Check row formula for k>=n-1:
       # Example n=5,k=4 (n-1): start=0,length=5, want f[4],f[3],f[2],f[1],f[0]
       # f[n-1:n-1-length:-1] = f[4:4-5:-1]=f[4:-1:-1] -> indices 4,3,2,1,0 good (stop -1 exclusive).
       # k=5: start=1,length=4, want f[4],f[3],f[2],f[1]? Constraints j=1..4: f[4],f[3],f[2],f[1]. f[4:0:-1] -> 4,3,2,1 good. Formula: n-1-length =4-4=0 -> f[4:0:-1]. Good.
       # k=8: start=4,length=1, want f[4]. f[4:3:-1] -> 4. n-1-length=3 -> f[4:3:-1]. good.
       A[ri,start:start+length] = f[n-1:n-1-length:-1]
       # For k<n-1 length=k+1, f[k::-1] uses stop None default 0. good.
       res=linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=M*ones, bounds=[(0,1e6)], method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': maybe 30?})`
```

       if res.status==0:
           g=res.x
           # clip tiny negative
           g[np.abs(g)<1e-12]=0; g=np.clip(g,0,1e6)
           S=res.x.sum()
           if S>0: obj=S
           conv=conv_fg(f,g)
           viol=conv > M*(1+1e-7)+1e-12
           if not viol.any(): return g,S
           # add top viol not active
           valid = [int(idx) for idx in np.where(viol)[0] if idx not in active_set]
           if not valid:
               # maybe due numerical; stop or tighten
               break
           # add top up to 64 by conv value
           vals=conv[valid]
           order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
           for idx in order[:min(64,len(order))]:
                lags.append(int(valid[idx])); active_set.add(...)
                if len(lags)>max_lags: break
       else:
           # maybe reduce constraints? break
    # return current g unscaled; if infeasible, scale down?
    # Ensure sum >0
    return g, max(S,1e-12)
```

Let's design the Fourier search more concretely:
```
def fourier_search(n, basis_dim, deadline, current_best, rng):
    K = basis_dim//2
    i = np.arange(n)
    t = i / n  # not n-1 to ensure DFT zero sum; end near 1
    B = np.empty((n,2K))
    for m in range(1,K+1):
       B[:,2*m-2]=np.cos(2π*m*t)
       B[:,2*m-1]=np.sin(2π*m*t)
    # Precompute cos/sin maybe using np.linspace? m up to K.
    def score_c(c, cache?): raw = 1 + B@c; raw[raw<0]=0; s=raw.sum(); if s<=0 return inf; p=raw/s; return fast_score(p)
    # Baseline: random uniform coefficient ranges. Use ranges varying.
    best_c=None; best_s=inf
    for _ in range(num_random):
       c = rng.uniform(-0.4,0.4, size=2K)
       # Bias first cos negative: c[0] -= 0.3? But if too negative raw nonneg; clip.
       s=score_c(c); update
       if s < global_best and time: local refine
    local refine: coordinate with step 0.05, try ±; also random perturb.
```
But if K=4 (8 coeff), random may not hit good. Use more structured: Start from optimizing one coordinate at a time from uniform. For each basis coefficient, find best 1D coordinate (bounded by nonneg) by golden section using fast score. This can find a good nonuniform. Then multi-coordinate random.

```

Helper functions:
```
def next_pow2(x): return 1 << (int(x)-1).bit_length()
def fft_conv(a,b,N):
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); B=A if b is a else np.fft.rfft(b,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)  # maybe norm='forward'? Default: irfft(fft?) yields n-length with no scaling? In numpy, ifft(fft(x)*fft(y)) gives convolution without scaling? Yes, default FFT unscaled forward, inverse scaled by 1/N, product yields convolution coefficients correctly? For np.fft: ifft(x) = (1/N) sum...; Conv theorem: ifft(fft(a)*fft(b)) = circular convolution. So correct.
    return c[:len(a)+len(b)-1]
```
Check scaling: For arrays product, no extra scaling. Example a=[1,1], b=[1,1], N=4: rfft? irfft squares gives [1,2,1,0]. Good.

Fast score:
```
def fast_score(p,N=None,n=None):
   if N is None: N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
   F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)
   b=b[:2*n-1]
   # due to numerical, max can be slightly > exact; we can use max(real)
   m=b.max()
   return 2*n*m  # sum p=1
```
In LP, we need conv of f and g. Use same.

Fourier search algorithm more detailed:
```
def fourier_search(n, K, deadline, rng, global_best_score, global_best_p, update_func):
    t = np.arange(n)/(n-1)
    B = np.empty((n,2*K))
    for m in range(1,K+1):
       B[:,2m-2]=np.cos(2πm*t)
       B[:,2m-1]=np.sin(2πm*t)
    N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    def c_to_score(c, p_out=None):
       raw = 1.0 + B.dot(c)
       np.maximum(raw,0,out=raw)
       s=raw.sum()
       if s <= 0: return inf, None
       p=raw/s
       sc=fast_score(p,N)
       return sc,p
    # We need update global best with exact? We'll update internal best_fast.
    best_c=zeros; best_sc=inf
    # Start from several presets:
    presets=[]
    # Random: use K maybe 5 (D=10)
    for trial in range(max(500, time?)):
       c = rng.uniform(-0.4,0.4,D)
       # optional bias: c[0] = -abs(c[0]) maybe
       sc,p=c_to_score(c)
       if sc < best_sc: best...
       if sc < global_best_score - tol: exact update?
    # local coordinate:
    c=best_c.copy()
    base_sc=best_sc
    max_iter=100
    for it in range(max_iter):
       improved=False
       for j in range(D):
          # sample offsets
          old=c[j]
          candidates = old + rng? step sizes [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]*sign? Instead deterministic: for delta in base_step*[1,-1,0.5,-0.5,2,-2]
          best_j=old; best_j_sc=base_sc
          for delta in ...:
             c[j]=old+delta
             sc,cand=c_to_score(c)
             if sc<best_j_sc: ...
          if improved update
          if time check break
       if not improved: base_step*=0.5; if step<1e-4 break
    # random basin: after local, do a few random c = best +噪声 and repeat.
```

Let's write LP code carefully.

```
def solve_lp_response(f, n, N, deadline, max_active=2500, time_per_lp=10.0):
    f_lp = f * n
    b = fft_conv(f,f,N)
    M = n * b.max()  # scaled
    if M <= 0: return f,1.0
    # initial lags
    zh = np.where(b > 0.5*M)[0]
    lags = list(np.unique(np.concatenate((zh, [0, 2*n-2]))))
    if len(lags) > 512: keep top 512 plus edges
    active_set = set(lags)
    rows = [make_row(k) for k in lags]
    g = f.copy()  # feasible? full yes
    best_obj = 1.0
    for inner in range(80):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30: break
       A = np.vstack(rows) if rows else None
       if A is None or A.shape[0]==0: break
       c = -1.0 - 1e-5 * f_lp  # maybe
       try:
          s0=time.monotonic()
          res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(A.shape[0], M), bounds=[(0, 10.0)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9, 'primal_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9})
          if not res.success:
             # try without dep? or break
             break
          g = np.clip(res.x, 0, None)
          S = g.sum()
          if S <= 0: break
          # evaluate violations
          conv = fft_conv(f, g, N)
          viol = conv > M + 1e-9
          if not viol.any():
             return g, S
          # If res is not improving objective vs f? It should S>=1 maybe.
          # Add top violations not active
          idxs = np.where(viol)[0]
          # prefer not active
          new_idx=[]
          vals = conv[idxs]
          order = idxs[np.argsort(vals)[::-1]]
          for k in order:
             if k not in active_set:
                new_idx.append(k)
             if len(new_idx)>=64: break
          if not new_idx:
             # maybe solver tolerance, scale g to feasible?
             scale = conv.max()/M
             if scale > 1:
                g /= scale
                S = g.sum()
             return g,S
          for k in new_idx:
             rows.append(make_row(k))
             active_set.add(k)
             if len(active_set) >= max_active:
                break
       except Exception:
          break
    # If incomplete, find a feasible g? We have g from last solve maybe infeasible.
    # Scale down if necessary
    conv = fft_conv(f,g,N)
    mx=conv.max()
    if mx > M:
       g = g * (M/mx)
       S=g.sum()
    else:
       S=g.sum()
    if S>0: return g,S
    return f,1.0
```
`bounds=[(0,10.0)]*n` upper bound 10? In original LP, g values could be >10? With f_lp values ~1 and M~1, each g constraint? A g_k≤M, if f has a value ~n (if f has spike), g could be small. Upper 10 likely safe? But to be safe, `ub = 1e4`. Too high may hurt solver. For n=4096, sum g optimum maybe up to n? But f sum1, if f has one large value ~1, M maybe ~? If f近乎 point mass, M≈1, f_lp at point n, constraints force g at neighbors ≤M/f≈1/n, sum small. If f flat, g sum≈1. So g values likely ≤O(1). Upper 100 enough. Use `ub=1e3`.

Rows function:
```
def make_row(k, f_lp, n):
    row = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if k < n-1:
        row[:k+1] = f_lp[k::-1]
    else:
        start = k - n + 1
        length = n - start
        row[start:] = f_lp[n-1:n-1-length:-1]
    return row
```

Local refine for a candidate:
```
def refine_c(c, sc):
   step=0.1
   for iter in range(80):
      improved=False
      for j in range(D):
         old=c[j]
         best=old; best_sc=sc
         for delta in (-step, step, -0.5*step,0.5*step, -2*step,2*step):
             c[j]=old+delta
             scj,pj=eval_c(c)
             if scj<best_sc: best_sc=scj; best=old+delta
         c[j]=best
         if c[j]!=old: improved=True
      if not improved: step*=0.5; if step<1e-5: break
   return c,sc
```
This coordinate line with fixed deltas may miss direction; random perturb also.

Now, row fill function with prealloc:
```
def fill_row(A, row, k, f_lp, n):
    A[row, :] = 0.0  # need clear because prealloc uninitialized
    if k < n-1:
        A[row, :k+1] = f_lp[k::-1]
    else:
        start = k-n+1
        length = n-start
        A[row, start:] = f_lp[n-1:n-1-length:-1]
```
Clearing entire row O(n) per added row. Total O(m*n), okay. To avoid full zero if row previously had values, we assign only used segment but need zero unused. We can set `A[row, fill_segment]` and zero rest. Simpler `A[row] = 0.0` then segment. O(n). For 2000*4096=8M assignments, fine.

For k<n-1, length k+1; zero at length:. For k>=n-1, zero at :start. Since row zeroed, no need separate.

In inner loop adding rows, `A[m]` row zeroed and filled; `m +=1`.

Initial active lags unique. If len > max_active, maybe keep top M. We can sort by b_lp descending and take max_active minus edges.

```
def initial_lags(b_lp, M_lp, n, max_active):
    if M_lp<=0: lags=[0]
    thresh = 0.5*M_lp
    idx=np.where(b_lp >= thresh)[0]
    # if too many, top (max_active//2)
    if len(idx) > max_active//2:
        vals=b_lp[idx]; idx=idx[np.argsort(vals)[::-1][:max_active//2]]
    lags = list(np.unique(np.concatenate((idx, [0,2*n-2]))))
    if len(lags)>max_active: lags=lags[:max_active]
    return lags
```

def _exact_score(seq):
    try:
       if not isinstance(seq, list): seq = [float(x) for x in seq]
       val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
       val = float(val)
       return val if np.isfinite(val) else float('inf')
    except Exception:
       return float('inf')
```

Let's define LP solver for small K:
```
def lp_response_full(q, K):
    q_scaled = K*q
    b = np.convolve(q,q)
    M_orig = b.max()
    M = K*M_orig
    # Build A rows
    A = np.empty((2*K-1,K))
    for k in range(2*K-1):
       row zeros; if k<K-1: A[k,:k+1]=q_scaled[k::-1]
       else: start=k-K+1; A[k,start:]=q_scaled[K-1:K-1-(K-start):-1]
    c = -1.0 - 1e-4*q_scaled maybe + 1e-6*rand? 
    res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2K-1,M), bounds=(0,1e3), method='highs')
    g=res.x
    return g/q sum? Actually g sum not normalized.
```
But building A each iteration from scratch O(K^2) with Python loops over 2K rows and slice assignments; for K=512, 1000 rows*512 ~0.5M assignments, fast. For K=2048 maybe 4M, still okay but fewer.

Since K small, we can use `np.convolve` for b and g convulations in objective? b length 2K-1; evaluating score of new q via `np.convolve` is O(K^2), K=512 ->262k, trivial. q = initial
for iter in range(max_iters):

   if best_sc < q_sc - 1e-10: q=best_new; q_sc=best_sc; no_improve=0 else no_improve+=1

Now, could the true optimum for K be lower than our LP iterations find? We can supplement with a direct coordinate descent / random local search in q-space for K up to 256. Local search can polish.

Direct local search for small K:
- q sum1 positive. Objective max coefficient. Use subgradient or random perturbations.
- Random perturb: q + ε * (r - mean(r)), clip, normalize. Use small ε 0.01-0.1. Accept if score improves. This is simple, each score O(K^2) via np.convolve. For K=256, 131k ops; 10k eval ~0.1s? maybe. We can run many.
- Coordinate moves: For each pair (i,j), transfer mass δ from j to i: q_i += δ, q_j -= δ. Use derivative at active max? Since K small, can evaluate a few candidate moves. But random likely okay.
- Use the active max lag k: gradient of b_k minus mean; move mass opposite gradient: q_new = q - step * (grad - mean(grad)). This is subgradient; for small K, can execute. Starting from nonuniform, not stuck. Use multiple active lags.
We can implement a simple `local_search(q, K, iters, deadline)`:
```
best=q; sc=score(q)
step=0.05
for it in range(iters):
   b=conv(best); m=b.max()
   active=np.where(b > 0.9*m)[0]
   # choose one active k (random or with max gradient norm)
   # grad for lag k: g[j] = K? derivative of b_k wrt q_j = 2 q_{k-j} (with q outside 0)
   g = zeros; idx j where 0<=k-j<K: g[j] += 2*q[k-j]
   if multiple active, average g over active or random weights.
   g -= g.mean()  # sum constraint
   # step size adaptive: step * norm(best)/norm(g)
   scale = step * best.std() / (||g|| + eps) maybe
   cand = best - scale*g; clip 0; normalize
   if score(cand) < sc: accept; no_improve=0 else reduce step / try random
   # also random perturb every 10 iter
```
But subgradient of max with active set may not be enough; could use `g = sum_{active} (b_k - 0.9m) * grad_k` weighted. Compute h = convolution of weight vector with q? For active weights λ length 2K-1, gradient = 2 conv(λ, q) (with linear convolution length). For small K, easy: `weights=np.zeros(2K-1); weights[active]=...; grad=2*np.convolve(weights,q)??` Need derivative for b_k: ∂b_k/∂q_j = 2 q_{k-j} for valid. This is 2 * (weights * reversed q?) Now, exact local score for q:
```
def local_score(q):
    # q nonnegative, sum maybe1
    s=q.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return inf
    b=np.convolve(q,q)
    return 2*K*b.max()/(s*s)
```

best_q=None; best_sc=inf

   q = generate_initial(K, rng, start)
   sc = local_score(q)
   # run LP iterations
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if time > deadline - 10: break
      g = lp_response(q, K, rng)
      if g is None: break
      # try t
      best_new=q; best_new_sc=sc
      for t in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0]:
          cand = (1-t)*q + t*g
          cand = np.clip(cand,0,None); cand/=cand.sum()
          cs = local_score(cand)
          if cs < best_new_sc:
              best_new_sc=cs; best_new=cand
      if best_new_sc < sc - 1e-10:
          q=best_new; sc=best_new_sc
      else:
          # try a small random perturbation of q to escape
          perturb = q + 0.02*(rng.normal(size=K)-mean); normalize; if score < sc accept else step decrease
          if no improvement 3: break
   if sc < best_sc: best_q=q.copy(); best_sc=sc
   # maybe polish with local random
   q,sc = local_polish(q,K,deadline)
   if sc < best_sc: update
return best_q,best_sc
```

def polish(q,K,iters=200,deadline):
   sc=score(q); step=0.05
   for it in range(iters):
      if time>deadline: break
      # random direction
      r = rng.normal(size=K) - r.mean()
      cand = q + step*r; cand=clip normalize
      cs=score(cand)
      if cs<sc: q=cand; sc=cs; step*=1.05? else step*=0.8
      # also active subgradient maybe every
   return q,sc
```

    repeats = np.arange(K)//(K/n)  # not for float; use np.interp
    q2 = np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,K), np.linspace(0,1,n), q)
q2 = np.maximum(q2,0); q2/=q2.sum()
```

Implementation of `lp_best_response(q, K, rng, dep=1e-4)`:
```
q = np.asarray(q)
q_old_sum = q.sum()
q = q/q_old_sum
q_s = q * K
b = np.convolve(q, q)
M = K * b.max()  # scaled RHS
A = np.empty((2*K-1,K))
# fill rows: zero not needed if we fill all entries? For row k<n, entries after k remain from previous? A new uninitialized, we must zero. Use np.zeros.
A = np.zeros((2*K-1,K), dtype=np.float64)
for k in range(K-1): A[k,:k+1] = q_s[k::-1]
for k in range(K-1,2*K-1):
    start = k-K+1
    length = K-start
    A[k,start:] = q_s[K-1:K-1-length:-1]
c = -np.ones(K) - dep*q_s
if rng is not None: c += 1e-6*rng.normal(size=K)  # maybe too random? Keep deterministic? small.
res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*K-1, M), bounds=(0.0, 1e3), method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
if not res.success: return None
g = np.clip(res.x, 0.0, None)
S = g.sum()
if S <= 0: return None
return g/S
```
But adding random dep may make objective not exactly feasible? still. If K=1024, random vector 1e-6 maybe negligible. Could omit random for reproducibility. Degeneracy of q upsample maybe solved by q bias. q bias favor high q; but if q has block structure, dep not random. Maybe okay. Add a deterministic small sinusoidal dep to break symmetry? Not necessary.

But other lags constrain. Upper 1000 should be enough for K≤1024. Use 1e4 to be safe. Large bounds might slow; use 1000.

LP objective dep `dep*q_s` adds up to dep*K maybe 0.1 if dep=1e-4,K=1024; too large vs objective 1. Use dep=1e-6? We want tiny. q_s ~1, dep sum ~dep*K? Actually ∑dep q_s_i g_i ~dep∑q_s g; if g sum1, q_s average1 => O(dep). So dep=1e-4 okay. Use 1e-5.

Let's define the final solution around the small-length insight:
- The exact score is homogeneous, so any optimal vector can be normalized to sum 1.
- For a nonnegative vector q of length K with sum 1, score = 2K max( q*q ).
- A length K vector can be upscaled by repeating each component L times into length KL with essentially the same score. This gives freedom to choose small K where dense LP and local search are cheap.
- Use a hierarchical schedule of K: 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512, and if enough time 1024. Start K=4 from a known good asymmetric vector [0.2,0.4,0.2,0.2] (score 1.92) and optimize each K with LP best-response and random local perturbations.

This avoids the previous failed large-n dense operator. Dense A for K=512 is only ~0.5M entries; for K=1024 ~2.1M, acceptable for limited iterations.

LP solver details:
- Use all constraints, not cutting plane, because K is small.
- Build A_{k,j}=K q_{k-j} when valid.
- RHS M=K max(q*q) (scaled score/2).
- Objective maximize sum(g) plus a tiny bias toward current q to reduce degeneracy: c=-1 - 1e-5*K*q? Actually q_s=K*q, so c=-1 - 1e-5*q_s. This keeps bias O(1e-5).
- Optional tiny random perturbation of c for different starts? For deterministic reproducibility, perhaps no random in objective; use different initial q instead. If the LP solution is degenerate and returns a poor extreme, the tiny current-q bias helps. Could also add a deterministic small sinusoidal bias to break symmetry, but not necessary.

Potential issue with LP degeneracy: For some q the LP may have multiple optimal g, and HiGHS may return one that does not improve. To mitigate, after LP, try several blends with the current q and also try a randomized LP solution if possible. Simpler: collect g from LP, and also use a smoothed version `g_smooth = w*g + (1-w)*q` before blending. Many blend factors t already smooth. If no improvement, local random perturbations can escape.

def _linprog_lp(c,A,b):
    try:
       return linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0,1000.0), method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
       return linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0,1000.0), method='simplex')
```

Now, code details.

`_score(np array q)`:
```
def _score(q):
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.clip(q, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = q.sum()
    if s <= 0.0: return inf
    b = np.convolve(q, q)
    m = b.max()
    return float(2.0 * q.size * m / (s*s))
```
This matches evaluator if no clipping needed. Use for internal. For internal fast, this is exact (dense). For K=1024, okay.

`_normalize(q)`:
```
q=np.clip(q,0,1e6)
s=q.sum()
if s<=0: q=np.full(q.size,1.0/q.size)
else: q=q/s
return q
```

`_build_a(q,k)`:
```
A=np.zeros((2*k-1,k), dtype=np.float64)
qs=q*k
for kk in range(k-1):
    A[kk, :kk+1] = qs[kk::-1]
for kk in range(k-1,2*k-1):
    start=kk-k+1
    length=k-start
    A[kk, start:] = qs[k-1:k-1-length:-1]
return A
```
Check second loop: kk from k-1 inclusive to 2k-2. start=0 for kk=k-1, length=k, `qs[k-1:k-1-k:-1]` = `qs[k-1:-k-1:-1]`, in Python stop negative? For k=4, qs[3:-5:-1] yields indices 3,2,1,0? Let's test mentally: stop -5 equivalent length-5=-1? Slicing with step -1 stops before -1 (index len-1?); qs[3:-1:-1] would yield 3 only? def make_initials(k, rng, prev):
    inits=[]
    if prev is not None: inits.append(upscale(prev,k))
    inits.append(np.repeat([0.2,0.4,0.2,0.2], max(1,k//4))[:k] normalized)
    for alpha in [0.3,0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0]:
       inits.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(k,alpha)))
    for trial in range(3):
       t=np.arange(k)
       raw=np.ones(k)
       for m in range(1,4):
           raw += 0.35*rng.normal()*np.cos(2π*m*t/k)
           raw += 0.35*rng.normal()*np.sin(2π*m*t/k)
       inits.append(np.maximum(raw,0)/sum)
    # maybe reversed, shifted
    return inits normalized
```

`local_polish` implementation:
```
def local_polish(q,k,rng, iters, deadline):
    q=normalize(q); sc=score(q); step=0.05
    for it in range(iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
       candidates=[]
       # random
       r = rng.normal(size=k); r -= r.mean(); r /= (np.linalg.norm(r)+1e-12)
       candidates.append(np.maximum(q + step*r,0))
       # active subgradient
       b = np.convolve(q,q)
       m = b.max()
       act = np.where(b >= 0.9*m)[0]
       if act.size:
           # choose weights top 8
           order=np.argsort(b[act])[::-1][:min(8,len(act))]
           ws=act[order]
           w=np.exp((b[ws]-m))*; w/=w.sum()
           grad=np.zeros(k)
           for kk,ww in zip(ws,w):
              # add ww * shifted q: for j, if 0<=kk-j<k value q[kk-j]
              start=max(0,kk-k+1); end=min(k-1,kk)
              # j range start..end, index in q = kk-j from kk-start down to kk-end
              grad[start:end+1] += ww * (2.0 * q[kk-start:kk-end:-1])
           grad -= grad.mean()
           gn = np.linalg.norm(grad)
           if gn>1e-12:
               cand = q - step * grad / gn
               candidates.append(np.maximum(cand,0))
       best_sc=sc
       for cand in candidates:
          norm=sum; if norm<=0 continue; cand=cand/norm; cs=score(cand)
          if cs<best_sc: best_sc=cs; best_cand=cand
       if best_sc<sc-1e-10:
          q=best_cand; sc=best_sc
          if step < 0.2: step *= 1.1
       else:
          step = max(0.002, step*0.7)
          if step <=0.0021: break
    return q,sc
```
The subgradient gradient scale: grad sum weighted 2q, mean subtract. Dividing by norm and multiplying step changes magnitude. Since q components ~1/k, grad norm ~? For k=128, q~0.008, grad~0.016, norm~0.18; step 0.05 => changes ~0.03, okay. For large k, q~1/k, grad norm~? ~sqrt(k)*2/k =2/√k, for k=512 ~0.09; step 0.05 small. Maybe don't divide by norm; use `cand = q - eta*grad` with eta adaptive. Simpler: `eta = step / (np.linalg.norm(grad)+1e-12) * (0.5*k?)`? Need not perfect; random helps.

Maybe simpler local polish only random perturbations with adaptive step. Since LP does the heavy work. Implement random only to save code:
```
for it:
  r=rng.normal; r-=mean; cand=q + step*r; normalize; if score<sc accept else step*=0.8
```
This is reliable. Add one subgradient candidate optional.

Given code length, maybe include random only. The LP already moves optimally in a sense. Random local can escape degeneracy.

```
def _optimize_start(q,k,rng,deadline,max_iters=60,polish=200):
    q=_normalize(q); sc=_score(q)
    stag=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 10: break
        t0=time.monotonic()
        g=_lp_response(q,k)
        if g is None: break
        best_q=q; best_sc=sc
        for t in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0):
            cand=(1-t)*q + t*g
            cand=_normalize(cand)
            cs=_score(cand)
            if cs < best_sc:
                best_sc=cs; best_q=cand
        if best_sc < sc - 1e-10:
            q=best_q; sc=best_sc; stag=0
        else:
            stag+=1
            if stag >= 5: break
        if t0 > 0:  # maybe if solve too slow, reduce? no
    q,sc=_local_polish(q,k,rng,max(polish, int(20*k)//? ), deadline)
    return q,sc
```

In `_local_polish`, step initial `0.05` maybe too high/low. Adaptive. Use exact score each candidate. For random, one candidate per iter. Also maybe every 5 iters try two different step sizes.

```
def _local_polish(q,k,rng,iters,deadline):
    sc=_score(q)
    step=0.05
    for _ in range(iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
       r=rng.normal(size=k); r-=r.mean()
       norm=np.linalg.norm(r)
       if norm: r/=norm
       cand=q + step*r
       cand=np.maximum(cand,0); s=cand.sum(); if s<=0: continue
       cand/=s
       cs=_score(cand)
       if cs < sc - 1e-11:
          q=cand; sc=cs; step=min(0.25, step*1.05)
       else:
          step=max(0.003, step*0.75)
          if step <=0.003: break
    return q,sc
```
This random walk uses normalized r; step is absolute change. For q sum1, enough.

Let's write `_linprog` robustly:
```
def _lp_solve(c,A,b):
    try:
       return linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, 1000.0), method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
       try:
          return linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, 1000.0), method='simplex')
       except Exception:
          return None
```
If `linprog` not defined, return None.

In `propose_candidate`:
```
best_list=None; best_score=inf
def consider(q, source=None):
   nonlocal best_list,best_score
   if q is None: return
   try:
     arr=np.asarray(q,dtype=np.float64)
     if arr.ndim!=1 or arr.size==0: return
     arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
     if not np.isfinite(arr).all(): return
     s=arr.sum()
     if s<=0: return
     arr=arr/s
     lst=[float(x) for x in arr]
     sc=evaluate_sequence(lst)
     sc=float(sc)
     if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<best_score:
         best_score=sc; best_list=lst
   except Exception: pass
```

          best_k_sc=sc; best_k_q=q
       consider(q)
    prev_q = best_k_q if best_k_q is not None else (starts[0] if starts else prev_q)

def _make_starts(k, prev, rng, max_starts):
    starts=[]
    if prev is not None:
       starts.append(_upscale(prev,k))
    starts.append(_tile_q4(k))
    # random dirichlet alphas
    for alpha in (0.3,0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0):
       if len(starts)<max_starts:
          starts.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(k, alpha)))
    # Fourier random
    while len(starts)<max_starts and len(starts)<max_starts+? no:
       t=np.arange(k, dtype=float)
       raw=np.ones(k)
       for m in range(1, min(5,k//2)+1):
          raw += 0.35*rng.normal()*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t/k)
          raw += 0.35*rng.normal()*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*t/k)
       raw = np.maximum(raw,0.0)
       if raw.sum()>0: starts.append(raw/raw.sum())
    # limit
    return starts[:max_starts]
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def _normalize(q):
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q) & (q > 0.0), q, 0.0)
    s = float(q.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(q.size, 1.0 / max(1, q.size), dtype=np.float64)
    q = q / s
    q = np.clip(q, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return q


def _score1(q):
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    if q.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    b = np.convolve(q, q)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    val = 2.0 * float(q.size) * float(b.max())
    return val if np.isfinite(val) else float("inf")


def _build_A(q, k):
    qs = q * float(k)
    A = np.zeros((2 * k - 1, k), dtype=np.float64)
    qr = qs[::-1]
    for kk in range(k - 1):
        L = kk + 1
        A[kk, :L] = qr[-L:]
    for kk in range(k - 1, 2 * k - 1):
        start = kk - k + 1
        L = k - start
        A[kk, start:start + L] = qr[:L]
    return A


def _lp_response(q, k):
    if linprog is None:
        return None
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    if q.size != k or q.size == 0:
        return None
    b = np.convolve(q, q)
    M = float(k) * float(b.max())
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return None

    A = _build_A(q, k)
    b_ub = np.full(A.shape[0], M, dtype=np.float64)
    c = -np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64) - 1e-5 * (q * float(k))

    res = None
    try:
        res = linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=(0.0, 1.0e6),
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True},
        )
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = linprog(
                c,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                bounds=(0.0, 1.0e6),
                method="simplex",
            )
        except Exception:
            res = None

    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None

    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g) & (g > 0.0), g, 0.0)
    s = float(g.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    g = g / s
    g = np.clip(g, 0.0, 1.0e6)
    s2 = float(g.sum())
    if s2 <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s2):
        return None
    return g / s2


def _local_polish(q, k, rng, iters, deadline):
    q = _normalize(q)
    best = q
    best_sc = _score1(q)
    step = 0.12

    for _ in range(max(1, int(iters))):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 3.0:
            break

        r = rng.normal(size=k)
        r -= r.mean()
        rn = float(np.linalg.norm(r))
        if rn > 0.0:
            r /= rn
            cand = q + (step / float(np.sqrt(k))) * r
            cand = _normalize(cand)
            sc = _score1(cand)
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best = cand
                best_sc = sc

        if k <= 512:
            b = np.convolve(q, q)
            m = float(b.max())
            if m > 0.0 and np.isfinite(m):
                act = np.where(b >= 0.9 * m)[0]
                if act.size:
                    top = act[np.argsort(b[act])[::-1]][: min(12, act.size)]
                    w = np.exp((b[top] - m) * 8.0)
                    wsum = float(w.sum())
                    if wsum > 0.0 and np.isfinite(wsum):
                        w = w / wsum
                        grad = np.zeros(k, dtype=np.float64)
                        for kk, ww in zip(top, w):
                            start = max(0, int(kk) - k + 1)
                            end = min(k - 1, int(kk))
                            if start <= end:
                                seg = q[int(kk) - end:int(kk) - start + 1][::-1]
                                grad[start:end + 1] += 2.0 * float(ww) * seg
                        grad -= grad.mean()
                        gn = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
                        if gn > 1e-14:
                            cand = q - step * grad
                            cand = _normalize(cand)
                            sc = _score1(cand)
                            if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                                best = cand
                                best_sc = sc

        # Acceptance / step update based on the best move this round.
        if _score1(best) < _score1(q) - 1e-12:
            q = best
            if step < 0.45:
                step = min(0.45, step * 1.05)
        else:
            step = max(0.005, step * 0.8)
            if step <= 0.0055:
                break

    return q, _score1(q)


def _optimize_start(q, k, rng, deadline, max_iters):
    q = _normalize(q)
    sc = _score1(q)
    stagnation = 0
    max_iters = max(1, int(max_iters))

    for _ in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 8.0:
            break

        g = _lp_response(q, k)
        if g is None:
            break
        g = _normalize(g)

        cand_best = q
        cand_sc = sc
        for t in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0):
            cand = (1.0 - t) * q + t * g
            cand = _normalize(cand)
            cs = _score1(cand)
            if cs < cand_sc - 1e-12:
                cand_sc = cs
                cand_best = cand

        if cand_sc < sc - 1e-10:
            q = cand_best
            sc = cand_sc
            stagnation = 0
        else:
            stagnation += 1
            if stagnation >= 5:
                break

    polish_iters = max(15, min(350, 50000 // max(1, k)))
    return _local_polish(q, k, rng, polish_iters, deadline)


def _upscale(q, k):
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    if q.size == k:
        return _normalize(q)
    if q.size == 0:
        return _normalize(np.ones(k))
    if k % q.size == 0:
        out = np.repeat(q, k // q.size)
    else:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, q.size)
        y = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, k)
        out = np.interp(y, x, q)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out) & (out > 0.0), out, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return _normalize(np.ones(k))
    return out / s


def _tile_q4(k):
    base = np.array([0.2, 0.4, 0.2, 0.2], dtype=np.float64)
    if k == 4:
        return base / base.sum()
    L = max(1, k // 4)
    out = np.repeat(base, L)
    if out.size < k:
        out = np.concatenate([out, base[: k - out.size]])
    out = out[:k]
    return _normalize(out)


def _make_starts(k, prev, rng, max_starts):
    starts = []

    if prev is not None:
        starts.append(_upscale(prev, k))

    starts.append(_tile_q4(k))

    if len(starts) < max_starts:
        for alpha in (0.3, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0):
            if len(starts) >= max_starts:
                break
            starts.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(k, alpha)))

    while len(starts) < max_starts:
        t = np.arange(k, dtype=np.float64)
        raw = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in range(1, min(5, max(1, k // 2)) + 1):
            raw += 0.35 * float(rng.normal()) * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t / float(k))
            raw += 0.35 * float(rng.normal()) * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * t / float(k))
        raw = np.where(np.isfinite(raw) & (raw > 0.0), raw, 0.0)
        s = float(raw.sum())
        if s > 0.0:
            starts.append(raw / s)
        else:
            starts.append(_tile_q4(k))

    return starts[: max(1, int(max_starts))]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    seed = int(seed)
    budget_s = max(5.0, float(budget_s))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(5.0, budget_s - 30.0)

    best_list = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    def consider(seq):
        nonlocal best_list, best_score
        try:
            arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
            if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
                return
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr) & (arr > 0.0), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(arr.sum())
            if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
                return
            arr = arr / s
            lst = [float(x) for x in arr]
            val = evaluate_sequence(lst)
            val = float(val)
            if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_score:
                best_score = val
                best_list = lst
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Use the supplied parent construction directly if it is available.
    try:
        if budget_s > 45.0:
            consider(globals().get("height_sequence_1", None))
    except Exception:
        pass

    # A short asymmetric seed already beats the uniform score 2.
    q4 = np.array([0.2, 0.4, 0.2, 0.2], dtype=np.float64)
    consider(q4)

    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 20.0:
        return best_list if best_list is not None else [1.0]

    prev = None

    # Most values: size, number of starts, LP iterations, max seconds per start.
    schedule = [
        (4, 2, 25, 10.0),
        (8, 3, 30, 15.0),
        (16, 3, 40, 20.0),
        (32, 4, 50, 30.0),
        (64, 4, 60, 55.0),
        (128, 3, 70, 90.0),
        (256, 3, 60, 130.0),
        (512, 2, 45, 180.0),
        (1024, 2, 30, 230.0),
    ]

    for k, max_starts, max_iters, max_alloc in schedule:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 25.0:
            break

        starts = _make_starts(k, prev, rng, max_starts)
        if not starts:
            continue

        k_best_q = None
        k_best_sc = float("inf")

        for i, q0 in enumerate(starts):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 15.0:
                break

            remaining = max(1, len(starts) - i)
            alloc = max(6.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) / float(remaining))
            alloc = min(float(max_alloc), float(alloc))
            if alloc < 6.0:
                break
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + alloc)
            if end - time.monotonic() < 3.0:
                break

            q_opt, sc_opt = _optimize_start(q0, k, rng, end, max_iters)
            if sc_opt < k_best_sc:
                k_best_sc = sc_opt
                k_best_q = q_opt

            # Keep the authoritative exact score updated periodically.
            if sc_opt < best_score + 0.05:
                consider(q_opt)

        if k_best_q is not None:
            prev = k_best_q
            if best_list is None or _score1(prev) < best_score - 1e-9:
                consider(prev)
        else:
            prev = starts[0]

    # Final safe fallback if something went wrong.
    if best_list is None:
        consider(q4)
    if best_list is None:
        best_list = [1.0]

    # Validate the returned candidate against the authoritative evaluator.
    try:
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(best_list))
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            best_list = _normalize(q4).tolist()
    except Exception:
        best_list = [1.0]

    # Headroom guarantee: ensure we return in time.
    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0 and len(best_list) > 4:
        q4_list = _normalize(q4).tolist()
        try:
            if np.isfinite(float(evaluate_sequence(q4_list))):
                best_list = q4_list
        except Exception:
            best_list = [1.0]

    return [float(x) for x in best_list]
```
